```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nFour generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Finding~2). Note that $\\tau$ was selected on the evaluation set; deployment would require a held-out validation split.\n\n",
```

```text
I found the paper! Here are the key details about arXiv paper 2604.07650:

**Title:** "How Independent are Large Language Models? A Statistical Framework for Auditing Behavioral Entanglement and Reweighting Verifier Ensembles"

**Authors:** Chenchen Kuai, Jiwan Jiang, Zihao Zhu, Hao Wang, Keshu Wu, Zihao Li, Yunlong Zhang, Chenxi Liu, Zhengzhong Tu, Zhiwen Fan, and Yang Zhou from Texas A&M University

**Submission Date:** April 8, 2026

**Core Problem:** The paper addresses whether seemingly diverse LLMs are truly independent, noting that shared pretraining data, distillation, and alignment pipelines can induce hidden behavioral dependencies that undermine multi-model systems like LLM-as-a-judge pipelines and ensemble verification.

**Key Finding:** In practice, apparent agreement often reflects shared error modes rather than independent validation, manifesting as correlated reasoning patterns and synchronized failures.

**Methodology:** The approach introduces a multi-resolution hierarchy with two information-theoretic metrics: a Difficulty-Weighted Behavioral Entanglement Index and a Cumulative Information Gain (CIG) metric.

**Results:** Experiments on 18 LLMs from six model families identified widespread behavioral entanglement, with CIG showing statistically significant association with degradation in judge precision.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Links: [{"title":"Correlated Errors in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.07962v1"},{"title":"[2506.07962] Correlated Errors in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07962"},{"title":"[2506.07962v1] Correlated Errors in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07962v1"},{"title":"Correlated Errors in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.07962"},{"title":"(PDF) Correlated Errors in Large Language Models","url":"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/392529896_Correlated_Errors_in_Large_Language_Models"},{"title":"[PDF] Correlated Errors in Large Language Models | Semantic Scholar","url":"https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/Correlated-Errors-in-Large-Language-Models-Kim-Garg/365ba5ab458c8ad6d4ed6996ab3e0ff3f386edbc"},{"title":"[Paper Note] Correlated Errors in Large Language Models, Elliot Kim+, ICML'25 · Issue #2145 · AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes","url":"https://github.com/AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes/issues/2145"}]

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's what the research is about:

**Paper Title:** "Correlated Errors in Large Language Models"

**Authors:** Elliot Kim, Avi Garg, Kenny Peng, and Nikhil Garg

**Overview:**
The researchers conducted a large-scale empirical evaluation on over 350 LLMs overall, using two popular leaderboards and a resume-screening task. They found substantial correlation in model errors—on one leaderboard dataset, models agree 60% of the time when both models err.

**Key Findings:**
- Factors driving model correlation include shared architectures and providers.
- Larger and more accurate models have highly correlated errors, even with distinct architectures and providers.
- The researchers showed the effects of correlation in two downstream tasks: LLM-as-judge evaluation and hiring—the latter reflecting theoretical predictions regarding algorithmic monoculture.

The paper was submitted to arXiv on June 9, 2025, and addresses concerns about homogeneity and diversity in large language models despite differences in training data, architecture, and providers.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Based on the search results, I found the paper arXiv 2510.01499. Here's what it's about:

## Paper Overview

The paper addresses how to effectively aggregate answers from multiple LLMs, noting that standard majority voting treats all answers equally, failing to consider latent heterogeneity and correlation across models.

## Main Contributions

The authors design two new aggregation algorithms called Optimal Weight (OW) and Inverse Surprising Popularity (ISP), leveraging both first-order and second-order information. While majority voting is a zero-order method that fails to account for latent heterogeneity (first-order information, e.g., LLM accuracies) and correlation (second-order information, e.g., inter-LLM prediction correlation) among models, these new methods address those limitations.

## Theoretical and Empirical Results

The theoretical analysis shows these methods provably mitigate inherent limitations of majority voting under mild assumptions, leading to more reliable collective decisions. 

The algorithms were empirically validated on synthetic datasets, popular LLM fine-tuning benchmarks such as UltraFeedback and MMLU, and a real-world healthcare setting ARMMAN. Across all 16 ensembles, OW-L outperforms majority voting in 97.92% of cases, while OW-I and ISP outperform it in 85.83% of cases, with majority voting never achieving the best performance in any case and absolute improvement in accuracy ranging from 0.54% to 14.20%.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Based on the search results, here's information about arXiv paper 2501.19306:

## Paper Overview

The paper is titled "SETS: Leveraging Self-Verification and Self-Correction for Improved Test-Time Scaling", authored by Jiefeng Chen, Jie Ren, Xinyun Chen, Chengrun Yang, Ruoxi Sun, Jinsung Yoon, and Sercan Ö Arık.

## Main Contribution

SETS (Self-Enhanced Test-Time Scaling) is a simple yet effective approach that overcomes limitations by strategically combining parallel and sequential techniques and fully leveraging LLMs' self-improvement abilities. It exploits the inherent self-verification and self-correction capabilities of LLMs, unifying sampling, verification, and correction within a single framework.

## Problem Addressed

Existing scaling methods have key limitations: parallel methods like repeated sampling are often inefficient and quickly saturate, while sequential methods like SELF-REFINE struggle to improve after a few rounds.

## Results

The paper demonstrates that SETS outperforms parallel scaling methods like repeated sampling and sequential scaling approaches like SELF-REFINE, achieving up to 10.9% accuracy improvement on the planning, reasoning, math and coding benchmarks with both non-thinking and thinking models (e.g., GEMINI-1.5-Pro and GEMINI-2.5-Flash).

The paper evaluates SETS on five challenging benchmarks: NATURAL PLAN, LiveBench Reasoning, MATH 500, AIME 2024-2025, and LiveCodeBench TestOutputPred.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0148CuzDNdSGCtDAmgzXjiWZ`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \citep{li2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	We distinguish three paradigms. \emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors even at this level. \emph{Program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \citet{chua2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \citet{zhang2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\kappa$ and $K_\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.
```

Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \\citet{chua2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \\citet{zhang2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\\kappa$ and $K_\\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.",

```text
Based on the search results, here's information about the paper arxiv.org abs 2510.01499:

**Paper Title:** Beyond Majority Voting: LLM Aggregation by Leveraging Higher-Order Information

**Authors:** Rui Ai, Yuqi Pan, David Simchi-Levi, Milind Tambe, Haifeng Xu

**Overview:** With the rapid progress of multi-agent large language model (LLM) reasoning, how to effectively aggregate answers from multiple LLMs has emerged as a fundamental challenge. Standard majority voting treats all answers equally, failing to consider latent heterogeneity and correlation across models.

**Main Contribution:** The work designs two new aggregation algorithms called Optimal Weight (OW) and Inverse Surprising Popularity (ISP), leveraging both first-order and second-order information.

**Key Details:**
- The authors highlight that majority voting, as a zero-order method, fails to account for latent heterogeneity (first-order information, e.g., LLM accuracies) and correlation (second-order information, e.g., inter-LLM prediction correlation) among models.

- Theoretical analysis shows these methods provably mitigate inherent limitations of majority voting under mild assumptions, leading to more reliable collective decisions.

- The algorithms are empirically validated on synthetic datasets, popular LLM fine-tuning benchmarks such as UltraFeedback and MMLU, and a real-world healthcare setting ARMMAN.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \\citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \\citep{li2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \\citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \\citet{zhang2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\\kappa$ and $K_\\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, wang2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. That $\\text{BR} > 1$ is expected from self-extraction; the finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \\citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \\citep{ai2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \\citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \\citet{zhang2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\\kappa$ and $K_\\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.",
```

```text
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\kappa$ and $K_\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.
```

_source line 18460 · 2026-05-21T08:47:28.669Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch] 两组新实验数据需要整合到论文：

**1. DRC FOLIO held-out 验证（加入 §6 DRC subsection）**

在 DRC 段落/表格中加入 FOLIO-204 结果（与 ProofWriter 并列对比）：

| Dataset | τ | w | Accuracy | Gain Recovery | NLI Cost |
|---------|---|---|----------|---------------|----------|
| ProofWriter | 0.7 | 3 | — | 90.0% | 38.8% |
| FOLIO | 0.7 | 3 | 58.33% | 83.3% | 34.8% |
| FOLIO | 0.8 | 3 | 58.82% | 100.0% | 48.5% |

FOLIO SC baseline = 55.88%, full combo w=3 = 58.82%。

关键叙事：DRC 在 held-out 数据集上 gain recovery 和 cost ratio 与 ProofWriter 高度一致，证明 per-question agreement routing 是 generalizable strategy。

**2. Process-conditional BR Decomposition（加入 §5 Analysis 或 §3 Method analysis）**

新增一段 Process BR 分析（2-3 句话 + 可选小表格）：

- Position: BR uniform ≈ 5.2-5.4 across early/mid/late reasoning steps (Fisher p=0.71)
- Difficulty: BR(easy, SC≥10)=18.8 vs BR(hard, SC≤6)=2.7 (p<0.001)
- 叙事：SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage — it is systematically present from the first reasoning step. Moreover, high-consensus errors exhibit dramatically amplified BR (18.8×), indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning.

数据文件位置：
- DRC FOLIO: `artifacts/drc_folio_results.json`
- Process BR: `artifacts/process_br_decomposition.json`

这次不用验证引用。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01S14NeAS5eYtH6uLgzvD55g`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\S\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
35	\label{sec:drc}
36	
37	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
38	
39	\begin{table}[t]
40	\centering
41	\small
42	\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\% of questions.}
43	\label{tab:drc}
44	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
45	\toprule
46	Method & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\
47	\midrule
48	SC only ($\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% \\
49	DRC $\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\% & 65/600 & 10.8\% \\
50	\textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} \\
51	DRC $\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\% & 455/600 & 75.8\% \\
52	Full combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% \\
53	\bottomrule
54	\end{tabular}
55	\end{table}
56	
57	DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\,pp out of $+6.67$\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Finding~2). Note that $\tau$ was selected on the evaluation set; deployment would require a held-out validation split.
58	
59
```

```json
      "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nFour generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$).   "new_string": "Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 On ProofWriter, DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost. Note that $\\tau$ was selected on the ProofWriter evaluation set; FOLIO serves as a held-out validation of the routing strategy.",
```
```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nFour generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Finding~2). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting; bias intensifies along reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}",
```
```json
    "newString": "Bias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nBias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting; bias intensifies along reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",
```

### Tool result

_source line 18490 · 2026-05-21T08:49:20.144Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016xgtUPp9oeKXkpvk66TtzG`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement across six open-source models (7B--32B), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two (both in the lowest-accuracy regime), and ten conditions remaining underpowered (none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$).
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap across six verifier-generator conditions ($p = 0.003$).
9	We further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at 39\% of verification cost.
10	
```

```json
    "type": "text",
    "file": {
      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
      "content": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement across six open-source models (7B--32B), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two (both in the lowest-accuracy regime), and ten conditions remaining underpowered (none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$).\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap across six verifier-generator conditions ($p = 0.003$).\nWe further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at 39\\% of verification cost.\n",
      "numLines": 10,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 10
    }
  },
  "sourceToolAssistantUUID": "032ad321-afc2-446e-902d-aca574565ffd",
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the confirmation bias when the verifier exceeds the generator ($p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 90\% of this gain at 39\% cost.
4
```

```json
    "oldString": "\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).",
    "newString": "\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% cost across two datasets (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work shows LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect} and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable \\citep{stechly2025selfverification}. Symbolic consensus introduces an additional assumption: that formalizing reasoning into first-order logic checked by a provably sound solver (Z3) provides \\emph{independent} verification evidence. Unlike natural-language self-correction, where generation and evaluation share the same neural channel, our formal channel is provably sound---the failure is precisely localizable to the extraction step. The assumption fails not because Z3 is unreliable (99\\% of constraints are syntactically valid; only 3.4\\% are rejected, \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), but because extraction inherits the model's biases, creating \\emph{formalized echo chambers}.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the confirmation bias: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Rather than discovering a new diversity--accuracy tradeoff \\citep{krogh1994neural}, we quantify its specific form in LLM self-extraction via three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$). Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a consistent monotonic capability-gap trend ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Um3ARi3UJFkBs2phB5aaKq`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}
5	\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. That $\text{BR} > 1$ is expected from self-extraction; the finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling.}
8	\looseness=-1 $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ \citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles.
9	
10	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
11	Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
12	
```
